# The directives of the fidelity suite that JavaScriptCore fails, run
# through the browser backend of the runtime layer in the MiniBrowser of
# the WebKitGTK lanes (`tests fidelity webkit`), WebKitGTK 2.54.1.
#
# The line shape is that of `expected-failures.txt`, V8's list:
# `<script>:<line> <citation>... <reason>`. The citations name what
# explains the failure: a defect of the engine, as a WebKit bug or a line
# of WebKit's source at a fixed commit, or a limit that the web embedding
# requires, as a line of the specification that sets it at a fixed commit.
#
# The lines of WebKit's source are at the commit whose trap messages the
# backend reads (`src/traps.rs`), and at the commit of the tag
# `webkitgtk-2.54.1` where the lanes' WebKitGTK differs from it.

# JavaScriptCore checks an indirect call to a null entry as a call to a
# function of another signature, in each tier: the interpreter at
# `WasmIPIntSlowPaths.cpp` lines 1203-1204, the baseline compiler at
# `WasmBBQJIT.cpp` lines 4954-4957, and the optimizing compiler at
# `WasmOMGIRGenerator.cpp` lines 6825-6840, in `Source/JavaScriptCore/wasm/`
# at the commit cited. Both throw `call_indirect to a signature that does not
# match`, which names two kinds, so the backend reports the trap as `Other`
# with that message, never a wrong kind. The suite's `uninitialized element`
# traps are most of these.

# The array instructions that read a segment answer one failure for each
# range they check, and each tier throws one message for it: the range of the
# segment, which Wasmtime calls an access out of the bounds of a memory or a
# table, and the range of the array, or an array too large to allocate.

# JavaScriptCore evaluates an expression of an element segment, such as a
# `struct.new`, again at each use: `initElementSegment` for `table.init` and
# `copyElementSegment` for `array.new_elem` and `array.init_elem`, in
# `Source/JavaScriptCore/wasm/js/JSWebAssemblyInstance.cpp` at the commit of
# WebKitGTK 2.54.1. The specification evaluates it once, when the module
# instantiates, so two uses of one element give the same reference.
array_init_elem.wast:157 https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/628d6ce1850e17225185a5807a9d0aa375bae2be/Source/JavaScriptCore/wasm/js/JSWebAssemblyInstance.cpp#L688-L695 JavaScriptCore evaluates an expression of an element segment again at each use, so two uses give different references

# The JavaScript API of WebAssembly, which the web embedding follows,
# defines exact limits that an implementation must refuse a module above
# with a `CompileError`, as V8's list records: every engine of the web
# refuses them.
table.wast:9 https://github.com/WebAssembly/spec/blob/608711107b7f1edb13efd57b7d79b49477462d36/document/js-api/index.bs#L2208-L2234 The embedding requires a table of at most 10000000 elements, and the module declares 4294967295

# The threads proposal makes an atomic wait on the web an `Atomics.wait`,
# and ECMA-262's DoWait throws where the agent cannot suspend, before it
# compares the value, as V8's list records. JavaScriptCore throws its
# message for an access out of the bounds of a memory there.
proposals/threads/atomic.wast:434 https://github.com/WebAssembly/threads/blob/cc535ada1aa21cfaa3cabf3ac73b89acef78a0a0/proposals/threads/Overview.md#L392-L401 https://github.com/tc39/ecma262/blob/726ec8a42625509026a6570f1d4649bfa9fe4156/spec.html#L47429-L47442 https://github.com/tc39/ecma262/blob/726ec8a42625509026a6570f1d4649bfa9fe4156/spec.html#L12770-L12780 The embedding requires `memory.atomic.wait32` on the main thread to throw, as `Atomics.wait` does, where the value differs and the wait returns at once
proposals/threads/atomic.wast:435 https://github.com/WebAssembly/threads/blob/cc535ada1aa21cfaa3cabf3ac73b89acef78a0a0/proposals/threads/Overview.md#L403-L412 https://github.com/tc39/ecma262/blob/726ec8a42625509026a6570f1d4649bfa9fe4156/spec.html#L47429-L47442 https://github.com/tc39/ecma262/blob/726ec8a42625509026a6570f1d4649bfa9fe4156/spec.html#L12770-L12780 The embedding requires `memory.atomic.wait64` on the main thread to throw, as `Atomics.wait` does, where the value differs and the wait returns at once
